# Candidate review — 2026-10-29

From `literature/candidates/2026-10-29.json` (472 discovery, 0 citing). Section boundary at `--min-score 3.0`; list A filters at `5.0`.

Tick one decision per candidate and fill in the notes, then run `python literature/03_record.py`. Accepting a citing paper proposes an `entity_paper` role; the `evidence_tier` that follows is derived by `scripts/05_validate.py`, never set here.

---

**33 candidate(s) are not in this file**, because a previous cycle rejected them. The decisions, with their reasons, are in `literature/decisions.yaml`.

## Discovery candidates (439)

New resources from the eight queries in `search_queries.yaml`. Scored for scope, with the table chosen by the query's `expect_type` — a database and a sequence model cannot share one (design §1.4).

**97 need a decision. 342 do not** — a candidate below `--min-score 3.0` is recorded as deferred rather than asked about. It is still written down in full: §1.5 measured that a filter discards more known-good papers than noise, and an unrecorded rejection returns as a candidate next cycle. What changed is only whose attention it needs, which is the same argument as list A's saturated roles.

## List A — cites the entity's primary paper (0)

Papers whose reference list contains a catalogued `primary` paper, scored at or above `5.0`. Naming the entity is worth up to 3.0 of that score but is no longer required: a record named after its own citation cannot be named in anyone else's prose, and the name gate reached 110 of the 283 curated evaluations where this one reaches all 283. The two lexicon sub-scores are printed next to every proposed role, because the split is sometimes a coin-flip (design §2.6.4).

**0 need a decision. 0 do not** — `evidence_tier` saturates, so once an entity has one independent evaluation no further row can move it. Author overlap decides which half a proposal lands in; it is still not an input to any score (§2.4). A proposal with no author list always needs a human, because independence cannot be computed from nothing.

### Needs a decision (0)

### Recorded without a decision (0)

Nothing here is dropped — each is recorded as the role shown, and `03_record.py` will attach it. They appear so the catalogue stays complete and so a curator can veto one, not so one has to be approved.

Every one of these names the entity. That is what makes the proposal strong enough to record unread: the reference gate only establishes that the paper cites the primary, which a paper does for many reasons. Rows with neither a name nor a tier to move are listed further down instead.

## List B — names a class, no catalogued model (0)

Deduplicated and global, because one such paper is evidence for several entities at once. Papers list A reported are left out, including the ones it only listed: since list A gates on the reference list, the two overlap where they used to be disjoint, and a curator should not be asked the same question twice — nor asked here what was deliberately not asked there. Each entry lists the catalogued primary papers it cites, so a curator can see which entities it might attach to.

What remains here that list A cannot reach is a paper citing no catalogued primary at all — found by class terms alone, so it has no reference-list anchor to any entity.

**0 need a decision. 0 do not** — the same saturation test as list A, applied across every entity the paper could attach to. One target whose tier could move is enough to need a human.

## Cited, not proposed (0)

Papers that cite a catalogued `primary` paper and score at or above `5.0`, but name no entity and cannot move one's `evidence_tier`. **Nothing here is recorded and nothing is asked.** They are written down so the sweep stays auditable and so a curator who wants one can attach it by hand.

Why they are neither: recording a role unread rests on the paper naming the entity, and asking about one that cannot move a tier spends a curator on a foregone conclusion — the two halves of list A above. A row with neither property satisfies the condition for neither half.
